=== 1 | Title Page ===

THE AENEID
by Virgil

=== 2 | [Latin] ===

THE AENEID OF VIRGIL

=== 1 | Book I ===

BOOK I
Arms, and the man I sing, who, forc'd by fate,
And haughty Juno's unrelenting hate,
Expell'd and exil'd, left the Trojan shore.
Long labors, both by sea and land, he bore,
And in the doubtful war, before he won
The Latian realm, and built the destin'd town;
His banish'd gods restor'd to rites divine,
And settled sure succession in his line,
From whence the race of Alban fathers come,
And the long glories of majestic Rome.
O Muse! the causes and the crimes relate;
What goddess was provok'd, and whence her hate;
For what offense the Queen of Heav'n began
To persecute so brave, so just a man;
Involv'd his anxious life in endless cares,
Expos'd to wants, and hurried into wars!
Can heav'nly minds such high resentment show,
Or exercise their spite in human woe?
Against the Tiber's mouth, but far away,
An ancient town was seated on the sea;
A Tyrian colony; the people made
Stout for the war, and studious of their trade:
Carthage the name; belov'd by Juno more
Than her own Argos, or the Samian shore.
Here stood her chariot; here, if Heav'n were kind,
The seat of awful empire she design'd.
Yet she had heard an ancient rumor fly,
(Long cited by the people of the sky,)
That times to come should see the Trojan race
Her Carthage ruin, and her tow'rs deface;
Nor thus confin'd, the yoke of sov'reign sway
Should on the necks of all the nations lay.
She ponder'd this, and fear'd it was in fate;
Nor could forget the war she wag'd of late
For conqu'ring Greece against the Trojan state.
Besides, long causes working in her mind,
And secret seeds of envy, lay behind;
Deep graven in her heart the doom remain'd
Of partial Paris, and her form disdain'd;
The grace bestow'd on ravish'd Ganymed,
Electra's glories, and her injur'd bed.
Each was a cause alone; and all combin'd
To kindle vengeance in her haughty mind.
For this, far distant from the Latian coast
She drove the remnants of the Trojan host;
And sev'n long years th' unhappy wand'ring train
Were toss'd by storms, and scatter'd thro' the main.
Such time, such toil, requir'd the Roman name,
Such length of labor for so vast a frame.
Now scarce the Trojan fleet, with sails and oars,
Had left behind the fair Sicilian shores,
Ent'ring with cheerful shouts the wat'ry reign,
And plowing frothy furrows in the main;
When, lab'ring still with endless discontent,
The Queen of Heav'n did thus her fury vent:
"Then am I vanquish'd? must I yield?" said she,
"And must the Trojans reign in Italy?
So Fate will have it, and Jove adds his force;
Nor can my pow'r divert their happy course.
Could angry Pallas, with revengeful spleen,
The Grecian navy burn, and drown the men?
She, for the fault of one offending foe,
The bolts of Jove himself presum'd to throw:
With whirlwinds from beneath she toss'd the ship,
And bare expos'd the bosom of the deep;
Then, as an eagle gripes the trembling game,
The wretch, yet hissing with her father's flame,
She strongly seiz'd, and with a burning wound
Transfix'd, and naked, on a

=== 2 | Book I ===

我歌唱戰爭與那個人，他為命運所迫，
受傲慢 Juno（朱諾）冷酷的恨意驅逐，
被迫離開特洛亞的海岸。
他歷經長久的勞苦，在海上與陸上皆然，
在勝負未決的戰爭中，在他贏得
拉丁人的國土、建立命定的城邦之前；
他使被放逐的神祇重返神聖的儀式，
並在他的血脈中確立穩固的繼嗣，
從而衍生阿爾巴諸王的族系，
以及莊嚴羅馬綿長的榮光。
啊，Muse（繆斯）！請述說起因與過犯；
何方女神被觸怒，其恨從何而來；
因何罪愆，天后開始
迫害如此英勇、如此正義之人；
將他焦慮的一生捲入無盡的憂患，
使他暴露於匮乏，並被驅入戰爭！
天上的心靈豈能懷此深重的怨懟，
或以人類的苦難來行使其惡意？
在台伯河口對面，但相距遙遠之處，
一座古城坐落於海濱；
那是 Tyre（推羅）人的殖民地；其民
剛勇善戰，並勤於經營技藝：
Carthage（迦太基）即其名；Juno 鍾愛此城
甚於她自己的 Argos（阿爾戈斯），或 Samos（薩摩斯）的海岸。
此地停駐她的戰車；此地，若天庭眷顧，
她意將其作為威嚴帝國的所在。
然而她曾聽聞一則古老的傳言流傳，
（長久以來為天上的居民所傳述，）
說未來的時日將見特洛亞族裔
毀滅她的迦太基，傾覆她的塔樓；
且不止於此，主權之軛
將加諸萬邦之頸。
她思量此事，並懼怕此乃命定；
亦無法忘懷她近來所發動的戰爭——
為希臘而對抗特洛亞國度之戰。
此外，長期的因由在她心中運作，
嫉妒的隱祕種子亦潛伏於後；
偏頗的 Paris（帕里斯）的裁判深深刻在她心中，
與她被蔑視的容貌；
被擄 Ganymed（伽倪墨得斯）所獲的恩寵，
Electra（厄勒克特拉）的榮耀，以及她受傷的床笫。
每一者皆為獨立之因；而眾因匯合，
在她傲慢的心中點燃復仇之火。
因此，遠離拉丁人的海岸，
她驅趕特洛亞軍的殘餘；
而七年漫長的歲月，那悲慘的流浪隊伍
被風暴拋擲，零落於大海之中。
如此時日、如此辛勞，為羅馬之名所需，
如此長久的勞作方能撐起如此宏大的基業。
如今特洛亞艦隊，憑風帆與槳，
幾乎已離開秀麗的 Sicily（西西里）海岸，
帶著歡快的吶喊進入水之國度，
在海面犁出泡沫翻湧的航跡；
此時，懷抱無盡的不滿，仍在煎熬，
天后如此傾洩她的怒火：
「我竟被征服了嗎？我必須屈服嗎？」她說，
「難道特洛亞人必將統治 Italy（義大利）？
命運如此安排，且 Jove（朱庇特）亦增其勢；
我的權能無法扭轉他們的坦途。
憤怒的 Pallas（帕拉斯）豈非曾以報復的惡意，
焚燒希臘的船隊，並溺死其士卒？
她，因一個冒犯之敵的過失，
竟敢擲出 Jove 自身的雷霆：
她以自下而起的旋風掀動船艦，
並使裸露的深海敞開其胸懷；
然後，如鷹擒住顫抖的獵物，
那可憐蟲，仍因父親的烈焰而嘶鳴，
被她強勢攫住，以灼傷之創
將其刺穿，赤裸地釘於
